A client that cannot read the library where it runs keeps a copy: Theme.swift, the iPhone app's Look.swift, look.js, the two web apps' app.css, the phone's setup page and the site's themes page. Each block of the library's colours in a copy sits between two comments, `design: evergreen light` and `design: end`. `internal/designcheck` lists the copies; `tools/design.sh` says where one is not the library's, `tools/design.sh sync` writes the library's values into every copy and into the themes page's tables, and `TestCopiesMatchTheLibrary` fails the tests when a copy was changed by hand. So a colour changes in the library, then here with `go get github.com/irie-people/roots@main` and one command.

The library also holds what Irie has learned about design: `principles.md`, each rule with its reason, its source and its check, and `components.md`, each part every client draws, its states, when to use it and where each client draws it; its `gallery.html` draws every role, state and part in each palette and mode. What comes next is in [proposals/design-library.md](proposals/design-library.md).

## 3. Harness support

### 3.1 Support levels

| Level | What works | What the harness must offer |
|---|---|---|
| **G**, generic | Launch in a pane host, freeze and thaw, alive/exited, notes typed in with bracketed paste and Enter | A command line |
| **O**, observed | Working, needs you (red) and done, from the harness's own events | Hooks, a plugin or an event stream that maps to the envelope |
| **H**, held | Pause at the next tool call; context notes at tool boundaries | A pre-tool hook that can wait and can inject text |
| **S**, subagents | A live read-only pane per subagent; per-subagent pause and notes | Subagent events with an id, plus a readable subagent transcript |

### 3.2 Capability matrix

In the Freeze column, "host" means `irie host` sends SIGSTOP to the agent's process tree. Ctrl+Z is never used: it is unreliable in Claude Code and undocumented for several others.

| Harness | Launch: model, and per-launch hook injection | Observe | Hold at the next tool call | Steer | Freeze | Subagents | Irie |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Code | `--model`, `--effort`, `--permission-mode`; `--settings <file>` | 33 hook events (command handler), JSONL transcript | PreToolUse and PostToolUse. The default 600 s timeout lets the call through; Irie sets `timeout` and `onFailure: "block"` (2.1.295+) | Hook `additionalContext`. Keys: Enter queues for the next tool boundary, Ctrl+X Ctrl+S sends now | host | SubagentStart/Stop; `agent_id` on tool hooks; `subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` | **v1: G O H S** |
| Codex CLI | `-m`, `-c k=v`; hooks via `CODEX_HOME` or `-c`, behind a trust gate (`--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` or pre-trust) | 12 hook events, rollout JSONL, OTel | PreToolUse, 600 s. Timeout outcome undocumented | Hook context. Enter injects; `codex queue`; app-server `turn/steer` | host | Child rollouts with `parent_thread_id`; SubagentStart/Stop | G now; full adapter first after v1 |
| Gemini CLI | `-m`; settings only via env (`GEMINI_CLI_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`) | 11 hook events, JSONL, OTel | BeforeTool. 60 s timeout, then the call goes through; raise `timeout` | Steering behind an experimental flag; Tab queues | host | `chats/<parent>/<sub>.jsonl`; no hook events | G; second wave |
| opencode | `-m provider/model`; `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` | Plugin bus, `opencode serve` SSE, SQLite | Plugin `tool.execute.before`, awaited, no timeout | Queue or abort only | host | SQLite `parent_id` | G; second wave |
| pi | `--provider`, `--model`; `-e <extension>` | Extension events, JSONL | `tool_call`, awaited | RPC `steer`; Enter steers | host | None built in | G; second wave |
| goose | `--provider`, `--model`; project plugin dir | Plugin hooks, SQLite, OTel | PreToolUse. 30 s timeout, then the call goes through (`on_failure` from 1.49) | Not documented | host | `parent_session_id` | G; third wave |
| hermes-agent | `-m`, `--provider`; `-p <profile>` or `HERMES_HOME` | Shell and plugin hooks, SQLite | `pre_tool_call`, which blocks the call on timeout (fails closed) | `/steer`; `session.steer` | host | Delegation logs, hooks | G; third wave |
| Amp | Modes, no model flag; project plugin dir | Plugins, `--stream-json` | `tool.call` plugin; timeout undocumented | Enter steers; `steer` flag | host | Server-side | G; third wave |
| crush | `-m`; project config | PreToolUse only; SQLite | PreToolUse. 30 s timeout, then the call goes through; top-level agent only | Not documented | host | `parent_session_id` | G; third wave. Its licence is FSL: run it, never copy its code |
| aider | `--model` | None (a notifications command, a history file) | Not possible | Ctrl-C, then type | host | None | G only |
| Any other CLI | Profile `command` and `args` | tmux only | None | Paste and Enter | host | None | G |

Every hook-capable harness except pi lets a held call through when its hook times out. Irie therefore sets each per-hook timeout above `hold.max`, and blocks on failure wherever the harness allows it. Those switches are Claude Code's `onFailure: "block"`, OpenHarness's `block_on_failure` and hermes-agent's `fail_closed`.

### 3.3 Claude Code adapter (v1)

**Launch.** Default profile:

```
claude --model claude-opus-5-5 --effort max --permission-mode auto \
       --settings <state>/sessions/<id>/claude-settings.json \
       [--agents <arch agents> [--agent <name>]] \
       [--mcp-config <state>/sessions/<id>/mcp.json] \
       --session-id <uuid> [--name <name>] "<task>"
```

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Task argument | Passed inline when it is at most 100 KiB, which keeps it under Linux's 128 KiB per-argument limit and macOS's 1 MiB total. Otherwise the argument is: `Your full task is in <path>/task.md. Read all of it before doing anything else.` A task that begins with `-` gets a leading space |
| Session id | Pre-assigned with `--session-id` (a v4 UUID), so Irie knows the vendor id before any hook fires and can reopen the session |
| Name | `--name` only with a name the user chose (§4.2). Without it Claude Code titles the session from its first prompt, with its small fast model, and writes each title to the transcript as an `ai-title` line, which the row's name follows. With it Claude Code writes `custom-title` and `agent-name` lines and no titles |
| Reopen (undo of close) | `claude --resume <uuid>` with the same model, effort, mode and settings flags, in the same cwd |
| Resume (move into Irie, §4.6) | `claude <profile flags> --settings <file> --resume <uuid>`: no `--session-id`, since a resumed session keeps its id, and no task, since it waits for input. `--name` is left out; the conversation keeps its own |
| Env | Adds `IRIE_SESSION`, `IRIE_SOCKET` and `IRIE_BIN`. Removes `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE` (`spawn.hide_tmux`), so an agent's own `tmux` commands cannot reach Irie's server. With agent teams (`spawn.teammates = "panes"`, §2.3) `TMUX` and `TMUX_PANE` name the session's anchor on the team server instead, the tmux shim's dir goes first on `PATH`, and `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` is set, unless the profile sets it; a `0` in the user's own settings still turns teams off. Removes the inherited variables that tie a process to another Claude Code session or terminal pane (§4.6), unless the profile sets them. A local profile also gets the environment that points Claude Code at its model (§6.1), under the profile's own `env` |
| Versions | `claude --version` must be 2.1.295 or later for `onFailure`. Older versions still spawn, but pause is disabled for that session and the row says why. Optional flags (`--name`, and the arch agents' `--agents` and `--agent`, §4.11) are detected from `claude --help` once per version; an unsupported optional flag is dropped with a warning, except `--agent` for a profile that runs as an arch agent, whose spawn then fails with the reason. `--append-system-prompt`, which only a tutor-mode session with `learn.visual` gets, is dropped without one. Irie's tips go in the settings file only from 2.1.247, which takes a tip with an id and a cooldown. Required values (model, effort, permission mode) are never dropped: if the CLI rejects one, the spawn fails visibly |
| `--settings` | From M2. The settings file adds hooks for this session only. Claude Code merges hook lists, so the user's own hooks still run. With `usage.status_line` it also sets `statusLine` to `irie statusline`; that key does not merge, so Irie's runs the user's own (§6.3). With agent teams it sets `teammateMode` to `tmux`. In a tutor mode (`learn.tutor`, [learning.md](proposals/learning.md)) it sets `outputStyle` to `Explanatory` or `Learning`, Claude Code's own styles, above the user's own files; off, it sets none, so a style the user chose stands. With `learn.agent_tips` it adds Irie's tips to `spinnerTipsOverride`, under the label `Irie tip`, each with a cooldown of 5 sessions. Claude Code reads the file once, when it starts. Claude Code passes the file on to split-pane teammates |
| `--plugin-dir` | Irie's skills (`internal/skills`, docs/proposals/skills.md), as a plugin named `irie` for this session only, whose skills run as `/irie:<name>`: a folder under `<data>/skills/plugins/`, named by a digest of what it holds, with read-only files. It is checked again before each launch and made again when an agent changed it, and the core removes folders no session uses when it starts. Passed only when `--help` lists the flag and `claude --plugin-dir <probe> plugin list --json` shows the probe's plugin loaded as `irie@inline`: managed settings may refuse the flag (`disableSideloadFlags`), and Irie's would stand in for a plugin of the user's own named `irie`. Otherwise the session's warnings say why. `skills.enabled`, `skills.disabled` and a profile's `skills` choose what goes in (§5.5); `irie skills` lists it |
| `--mcp-config` | Irie's own MCP servers for this session only, in `sessions/<id>/mcp.json` as `{"mcpServers": …}`: Irie's browser, `irie-browser`, while `browser.connector` is on and the browser is found (§4.15), and for an anonymous research session `irie-anon` with the browser through its exit (§4.16). The user's own servers still load, but in an anonymous session, which also gets `--strict-mcp-config` and `permissions.deny` for WebFetch and WebSearch in its settings file, and fails to start on a CLI without them. Passed only to a CLI whose `--help` lists the flag, and not with a profile that passes its own. The flag takes several files, so another flag always follows it, never the task |
| `--append-system-prompt` | In a tutor mode with `learn.visual`: one paragraph that asks the agent to draw a small Mermaid diagram where a picture helps the user follow an explanation (`harness.VisualNote`). It goes after Claude Code's own instructions and the output style, and takes nothing away |

**Hooks in `claude-settings.json`.** Every hook uses `{"type":"command","command":"<IRIE_BIN> hook"}`.

| Event | Matcher | Timeout | `onFailure` | Irie use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SessionStart | none | 10 s | none | Started; transcript path; the delegation note (§6.4) and the user's preferences (§4.2); queued notes |
| UserPromptSubmit | none | 10 s | none | Working; notes for an idle agent |
| PreToolUse | `*` | `hold.max` + 120 s | `block` | Tool started; hold; AskUserQuestion and ExitPlanMode mean "needs you"; notes |
| PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure | `*` | `hold.max` + 120 s | none | Tool finished; hold; notes |
| PermissionRequest | `*` | 10 s | none | Needs you (permission). Never answered |
| Notification | none | 10 s | none | `permission_prompt` and `agent_needs_input` mean "needs you" |
| Elicitation | none | 10 s | none | Needs you |
| Stop | none | 10 s | none | Turn ended; the jot dog may send it on (§4.8) |
| StopFailure | none | 10 s | none | Turn failed: `rate_limit`, `authentication_failed`, `billing_error`, …; a model's own usage limit may switch the session's model (§6.4) |
| SubagentStart, SubagentStop | none | 10 s | none | Child rows and mirror paths |
| SessionEnd | none | 1 s | none | Session ended (Claude Code allows 1.5 s by default) |
| PreModelSwitch, PostModelSwitch | none | 10 s | none | Irie's own switch at a usage limit skips the confirmation; the model a session runs (§6.4). Only for a Claude Code of 2.1.295 or later |

An arch agent's door (§4.11) adds prompt hooks to PreToolUse beside Irie's own: one per rule, such as `{"type":"prompt","if":"Bash(curl *)","prompt":"…$ARGUMENTS…","timeout":20,"continueOnBlock":true}`, grouped by the tool the rule names.

**Payload fields Irie relies on.** The fake agent used in tests must produce exactly these.

| Field | Events | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `hook_event_name`, `permission_mode` | all | Vendor id, tailer, sanity checks, display |
| `agent_id`, `agent_type` | Tool events fired inside a subagent; SubagentStart/Stop | Per-subagent hold, child rows |
| `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id` | PreToolUse, PostToolUse | Detail line; question detection |
| `notification_type`, `message` | Notification | Reasons for "needs you" |
| `error`, `error_details`, `last_assistant_message` | StopFailure | Error reason; what the API said of it, and the error as the conversation shows it ("API Error: …", "You've reached your Fable limit. …"), kept in the journal |
| `from_model`, `to_model`, `requested_model`, `source` | PreModelSwitch, PostModelSwitch | Whether a switch is Irie's own; the model a session runs from then on |
| `tool_response` (`stdout`, `stderr`, `interrupted`); `error`, `is_interrupt` | PostToolUse; PostToolUseFailure | What a shell command's tests showed (§4.8). The shim keeps their head and tail |
| `last_assistant_message`, `stop_hook_active`, `background_tasks`, `session_crons` | Stop | The turn's result (§6.4); whether the jot dog may send it on (§4.8) |
| `agent_transcript_path` | SubagentStop | Confirms the mirror's path |
| `reason` | SessionEnd | Why the session ended |

**Replies.** The shim always exits 0.
- **PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure, UserPromptSubmit, SessionStart, SubagentStart:** print `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"<event>","additionalContext":"<notes>"}}` only when there is something to say; otherwise print nothing.
- **PreToolUse after `hold.max`:** print `permissionDecision: "deny"` with a `permissionDecisionReason`.
- **Stop:** when the jot dog sends the turn on (§4.8), print `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"Stop","additionalContext":"<what is left>"}}`: feedback that goes on with the turn, under Claude Code's own loop guards. Otherwise print nothing.
- **PreModelSwitch:** `permissionDecision: "allow"` for the switch Irie makes at a usage limit (§6.4), so it goes ahead without the confirmation Claude Code asks while the prompt cache is warm. Any other switch, such as the user's own `/model`, gets nothing, and Claude Code decides as it would.
- **Never `"allow"` a tool call:** that would bypass the user's permission rules.
- **All other events:** print nothing.

**While no core listens.** For the seconds an install takes to restart the core (10 to 30 s with some 40 sessions), the shim cannot connect, and an event it dropped then was lost: a turn that failed in that window left its row "working" while its agent waited at its prompt. So when the shim cannot connect it appends the event, as the core would have got it, to `sessions/<id>/missed.ndjson`, one line each, `{"at","harness","payload"}`, in one write, and only when the session's folder is there. Past 1 MiB it keeps only the events that change the session's state, not its tool calls. Each tick the core takes each live session's file once nothing has been added to it for a second, and records its events in the order they came, at the time they came and with `late` in their data: the same as a hook, but for what only a reply carries (the jot dog's nudge, the trod bot's answer, the arch agents' word, Gabriel's guard, a note), which reaches no agent now. An event no later than the last one the core heard from the session's agent is left out, as the agent went on since, and so is one from before the session last started, as a reopen starts it again; the core weighs that as it records the event. A core that stops closes its socket first, so a hook that comes then is kept too. An event whose reply the shim waited for in vain, the core has; only one the shim could not hand over is kept.

### 3.4 Adapter interface

```go
// internal/harness
type Harness interface {
	ID() string    // "claude-code", "generic"
	Levels() Levels // G, O, H, S flags
	// Launch returns argv, extra env and files to write (e.g. a settings file) for a new session.
	Launch(p config.Profile, s Spawn) (argv []string, env map[string]string, files map[string][]byte, err error)
	Reopen(p config.Profile, s Spawn) (argv []string, env map[string]string, err error)
	// Normalize turns one raw hook payload into envelope events (§5.1).
	Normalize(raw []byte) ([]event.Event, error)
	// Respond renders the core's reply in the harness's own hook output dialect.
	Respond(e event.Event, r HookReply) []byte
	SubagentTranscript(parent event.Event, agentID string) string
}
```

### 3.5 Sessions on the Agent SDK

A Claude plan's monthly API credits pay for the Claude API, Managed Agents and the Agent SDK, but not for Claude Code. A key whose organization holds only those credits gets "Credit balance too low" in every Claude Code session. A profile with `sdk = true` runs its sessions on the Agent SDK instead, which those credits cover. The Agent SDK's CLI is `claude -p`, and its docs say to run it as a subprocess from a language other than Python or TypeScript. Irie does exactly that, with no change to what the CLI says it is.

**Launch.** The adapter builds the same command line as for Claude Code's terminal app, without the task, and puts Irie's own pane program in front:

```
irie sdk [--first <state>/sessions/<id>/task.md] -- claude --model … --settings … --session-id <uuid>
```

`irie sdk` (`internal/sdk`) adds `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --permission-prompt-tool stdio` and runs the CLI as its child. The task file's text is the first message. A resume passes `--resume <uuid>` and sends `--first` only for a fork's next prompt.

**What it does.**

| Part | How |
|---|---|
| Messages | Each one goes in as a JSON line `{"type":"user",…}`. One sent while a turn runs reaches the agent within that turn, as a message queued in Claude Code's terminal app does |
| The terminal | The log scrolls up: the user's messages in bold; the reply as it streams, a whole line at a time, wrapped at spaces; each tool call faint, with its command or file, and its result in a few words; then the turn's time and the session's cost. The bottom row is the line being typed. Raw mode, with bracketed paste, so the core types a message as it does to Claude Code: a paste, then Enter |
| Keys | Enter sends. Escape stops the turn: a `control_request` of subtype `interrupt`, whose turn ends as an error with the CLI's diagnostic, shown as "stopped". Ctrl+C stops the turn too, clears the line, or, twice, ends the session. Ctrl+D on an empty line ends it: the CLI's input closes and it exits after its turn |
| Permission prompts | They come as `control_request` `can_use_tool`. The full call shows in the log, then `Allow <tool>: <what>?` with `❯ 1. Yes` and `2. No`, numbered as Claude Code numbers its menus, so the phone app's keys answer it too (§4.7). Yes sends back the call's input; No denies it and ends the turn. The PermissionRequest hook still fires, so the row says "needs you" while it waits |
| Questions | AskUserQuestion is denied, with a note asking the agent to put its question in its reply |
| Hooks | `--settings` hooks run under `-p`, so Irie observes, holds and steers the session as any other (§3.3) |
| Cost and context | Claude Code's status line does not run under `-p`. Each turn's result carries the session's cost; the context is the last request's tokens over the model's window. `irie sdk` sends both to the core as a status line would (§6.3) |
| Freeze, close | The CLI is the host's grandchild, in the same process group; freeze stops the host's whole tree (§4.3), and a close's signal reaches both |
| Exit | The pane ends when the CLI does, with its exit code; what it wrote to stderr shows above |

**What it goes without.** The parts of Claude Code's terminal app: its status line, tips under the spinner, slash-command menus, agent teams' panes and the question form. Under `-p` a folder is never asked to be trusted, and a project's hooks and MCP servers run at once; Irie starts sessions only in the user's own repos and folders. The agent loop, tools, skills, subagents, sessions and transcripts are Claude Code's own, so the conversation view and reopen work as for any session.

**Where.** A machine whose Claude Code is signed in to such an organization, with `claude auth login --console`, sets `sdk = true` on its Claude Code profiles. Its Iries run every session there on the credits.

---

## 4. v1 must-haves

### 4.1 Session list

The sidebar is a terminal program that reads like a web page, and like the Mac app: rounded cards with padding, a tree of repos, plain words, and a mouse that lights up what it points at. It paints every cell in the palette of `ui.theme` (§5.3, §5.5).

**Layout, from top to bottom:**
- **Header** (pinned, 1–2 lines): a title bar on the palette's ground, a shade deeper than the sidebar: `Irie` and its tagline while there is room, then counts (`◐3 working  ●1 needs you  ✓2 to verify  ‖1 paused  ◆0 frozen`, cut down to glyph and number when narrow), then, on a second line from 10 rows, the undo and redo labels from M4 (`↶ rename "api-fix"  ↷ –`), each cut to its share of the width when narrow. Needs you counts every red row (needs you, stalled and failed), working counts the sessions that wait on their helpers too (§5.3), to verify leaves those out and the parked sessions, paused counts pausing too, and a zero count is faint. When the core is unreachable the header says `core offline` and the UI retries every second.
- **Tabs** (1 line, from 10 rows): `List │ Repo`, the view in use on the selection fill. A click on a tab, or `v`, switches. The sidebar opens in Repo.
- **List:** scrolls. Groups of sessions, each under a header that folds it.
- **Status strip** (1 line): the selected row's detail in plain words ("Running the tests", "Asks: may it run rm -rf build?"), with the call it is about ("Bash · go test ./..."), what it costs and its name, and its folder on the right while that leaves the rest a third of the line; or the last error. A row's `detail` is the plain words the core records on each tool event (`phrase`), and its `command` is the call; a journal line from before the phrases gives the call as the detail. From 24 rows it is a **details card**: a rounded card with the selected session's name in its border, then its state with what it costs and how it was routed, its detail, its exact call, and its branch and folder. An error or a message takes the card's four lines.
- **Command bar** (pinned): from 10 rows a rounded box, edged in the accent while it has the keyboard, with the placeholder "What should we work on?". It grows from 1 to 8 lines with the input, then scrolls inside itself. The terminal's own cursor marks the place, so a screen magnifier or reader can follow the typing.

**Groups.** The List view is one list, with no header but your groups' (below). The Repo view has a group per repo (a session's `repo`, its main checkout), named by the repo's folder, with parent folders added when two repos share a name (`code/irie`, `forks/irie`), in the order their first session started, then "Not in a repo". In both, the parked sessions wait in the Parked group after the rest, folded until you open it, and a paired machine's sessions (§2.8) in a group of that machine's own after Parked, `On Asahi`, in the order they have there. A header reads `▾ irie  4`; Enter, Space or a click folds it to `▸ irie  4 · 1 needs you`, which counts in red what inside needs you. Within a group sessions follow the core's one order (below), so a state change never moves a card: it changes only the card's glyph and colours. A finished turn stays where it is, marked *to verify* (§5.3), until you have checked its work and park or close it (§4.5). Parked, it is no longer to verify: it says *Parked* under a quiet name and leaves the count. An earlier State view grouped the sessions into "Needs you", "Working" and "To verify", which moved a card whenever its state changed; the List view replaced it.

**Cards.** Each session is a rounded card a column in from each side of the pane, with a column of padding: the state's glyph, any flag beside it (§5.3: `◆` for a frozen session under a state that comes first), an `↑` in the accent for a boosted session, the name and the age on its first line, its detail in plain words on the second (red when it needs you, and the state in words when it has none), then up to three of its running helpers, `↳ Explore  Reading DESIGN.md`, and `and N more`. A card that needs you is tinted and edged in red. The selected card takes the selection fill, edged in the accent while the list has the keyboard; the card under the pointer takes the hover fill. Where the list has fewer than 8 rows, or the pane fewer than 24 columns, each session is a one-line row instead, `[glyph][↑] name … age`, in the same palette, the selected one with an accent edge. Sessions outside Irie are always one-line rows. Subagent rows (M8) sit in their parent's card, as its helpers.

**Order.** The core keeps one order, which both sidebars draw and each row carries as `order`: a new session goes where `ui.new_tabs` says, last (the default), first among the rest, just under Pinned, or `next`, just after the session that started it, as a browser opens a tab beside the one it came from: the session whose agent ran `irie ctl spawn`, or the one selected where the task was typed (with none, last; from a pinned one, first among the rest). Dragging one with the mouse puts it where it is dropped (`reorder`); a boost never moves one (§4.3). The order lives in the `@irie_order` option of the `irie-agents` session, so a restarted core keeps it, and a closed session keeps its place for its reopen. Each group draws its sessions in that order, and the groups keep their own order. A card or row dragged with the mouse moves among the others of its group as the pointer crosses them, in either view, and only once the pointer is where it would land, so cards of different heights never trade places back and forth; dropped, it stays, and dropped on another group it goes back. In the Mac app the cards stay as they are until the drop, and a line shows where the card would land (Your groups, below). Held near the list's top or bottom edge, a drag scrolls the list that way, so a card goes past the ones out of sight: in the Mac app faster the nearer the edge, from a band a fifth of the list deep (64 to 120 points, about two cards), easing into each new speed so it never lurches, and over the find field above the list at its fastest; in the terminal sidebar an entry at a time while it holds on the list's first or last two lines, or past them, faster on the edge's own line, carrying the row along. An edge the drag began on waits until the pointer moves, so a card taken up there doesn't set the list off. State changes never reorder a group, and only you scroll the list: frames from the core and a growing command bar leave what is on screen where it is, and only a task you just started scrolls into view, its group unfolding. The Parked group comes after the groups. Parking a session (`z`, `park`) moves it there at once, last in Parked, and unparking it puts it last among the rest, just above Parked, where the eye already is, rather than back in a place far up a long list: both move it to the end of the one order, and undo puts it back where it was. Boosting a parked session leaves it in Parked (§4.3). A card or row dropped across the Parked group's edge is parked, or unparked, where it lands, in one `park` that names the session it lands before, as long as it would land in that group: any session can go into Parked, and a parked one comes back only into its own group. Park is organisation only: a parked session keeps running and still turns red when it needs you (§10, decision 3). Parked starts folded, so a session parked goes out of sight at once, and the selection on it goes to the header; once opened, it stays open, and the selection goes with a session parked into it. Its header reads `▸ Parked 4 · 1 needs you` and turns red when any parked session needs you; expanded, it shows its rows. Each paired machine's group comes after Parked, and the Outside Irie group (§4.6) last; both fold like the others. Red marks only the rows that need you (§5.3); each state also has its own glyph, so red is never the only cue.

**Pinned.** Parked's opposite: the Pinned group heads the list, in both views, and holds the sessions you keep at hand, such as a long-lived orchestrator. Pinning a session (`pin`, `:pin`) puts it last in Pinned, and unpinning it first among the rest, just under Pinned, where the eye already is; a card dropped across Pinned's edge is pinned or unpinned where it lands. A session is pinned or parked, never both: pinning a parked one unparks it, and parking a pinned one unpins it, and undo puts both back. No close reaches a pinned session but your own of it alone: Close Others, Close Below, Close All Finished and a group's Close Group pass it over, and so does `spawn.close_when_done`. Pins live in the journal (`ui.pinned`, `ui.unpinned`), as parks do. Only this machine's sessions pin.

**Send to Top.** A task sent to the top (`top` in `spawn` and `input`: the Mac app's Send to Top, `irie ctl spawn --top`, `irie ctl input --top`) starts pinned and first in Pinned, over every card, so first in the list of its space, and boosted (§4.3), as your own pin and boost would leave it, both in the journal. The boost lasts until you stop it, as a card's Boost does, so every other session steps back until then, even once its turn is over. Its start is its one step of the history, whose undo closes it; the card's Unpin and Stop Boosting take back the rest. It is a task of its own however it reads, so it never adds to the jot before it (§4.2), though the jot after it may add to it; and it stays on this machine, where it pins, when `place.mode` would start it on a peer (§2.8). The reply to a typed one says so: "It's at the top, first in Pinned, and boosted." A core that takes it lists `spawn-top` in `hello`. The owner asked for it on 2026-11-21: a jot that goes to the top of Pinned with its priority on.

**Spaces.** Named sets of sessions that a sidebar shows one at a time, like a browser's spaces: say Irie, Steak and Gigbot. Each has its own Pinned and Parked, since a card is in one space only, and every machine's group and Outside Irie show in every space. Every session is in one: the first, unless you put it in another (`space`), or it started from a session in another, as a new session takes the space of the one that started it (`after`, `parent` or `continues`), or the space the client shows where the task was typed (`space` in `input`). The spaces live in the `@irie_spaces` option of the `irie-agents` session, in their order, and a session's space in the journal (`ui.spaced`), as its park does, so a restarted core keeps both and a closed session keeps its space for its reopen; a session whose space is gone is in the first. Moving sessions to another space is one step of the history; making, renaming, moving and removing a space are not. The title bar's counts count every space, and showing a session in another space takes the sidebar there. Close All Finished takes the space on show. The Mac app shows the spaces as chips under the list (⌃1–9 shows one, ⌃⌥1–9 sends the selected or picked cards to one, and a card or a group of yours dropped on a chip moves there, or dropped on the + after them, which reads New Space while a drag holds something, to a new space, named after a group); the terminal sidebar shows every space's sessions for now.

**Your groups.** Named sets of sessions that the List view draws together, under a header that folds them, like a browser's tab groups: say Steak, for the cards of one goal. Groups hold groups too, as folders hold folders, up to eight deep: Work holds Irie, which holds Sidebar, each drawn inside the one around it, a step further in with a rail of its own, and its header counts every card within it. A card's menu makes a group of it, or of every card picked with it, inside the group that holds them all, as a new folder of files picked in a folder goes in that folder, and names it at once; it moves a card to any group, listed as a tree, or one group out of its own (`group`, `:group [NAME]`, `:ungroup`, `irie ctl group`). A header's menu puts its group in a new group, moves it into another group or one out of the group around it, or to another space or a new one named after it, whole (`space` with `groups`), and ungroups it, which leaves what it held where it is, in the group around it (`group` with `move`, `:nest [NAME]`, `irie ctl nest`). Names are unique among the groups beside each other, as files in a folder are, and a path such as `Work/Irie` names a group inside another, finding or making each group of it (`:group`, `irie ctl group`, `irie ctl spawn --group`). A group has no place of its own in the order: a sidebar draws it where the first of its sessions is, those of the groups inside it included, with its other sessions after it in their order and the groups inside it where the first of theirs is, and the sessions of no group between your groups with no header. So the core keeps a group's sessions together, as that tree draws them, whatever changes the order, which never changes what a sidebar shows: a new group forms where the first of what it holds is, a session or group that joins goes last in it or where a drag dropped it, and one taken out goes just after the group it left. In the Mac app a card dropped among another group's cards or on its header joins it, and one dropped among the cards of none leaves its own; a header drags its group with it, which lands as a card does. A drop goes between the two rows the pointer is between, a line showing where, as far in as the group it would join, or inside a group by its header, first in it when the group is open and last when it is folded, which a drag held there springs open after a moment; where a group ends, the row under the pointer picks between last in the group and after it, in the one around it, and the pointer's travel to the side, a step every 24 points, picks among the groups ending there, left further out and right further in; under the last card the drop goes to the top of the list, last. A group never goes into itself, into one of its own groups, or deeper than eight. The keys move a card only within its outermost group. A pinned or parked session of a group waits in Pinned or Parked and comes back to its group: unpinned or unparked it goes last in the group, so the group stays where it is; one that joins a group comes out of Pinned or Parked. A session an agent starts goes in that agent's group (`--group` names another, made when none has the name), and so does a task typed beside a card of a group with `ui.new_tabs = "next"`. Each space has its own groups: a session moved to another space leaves its group, unless the whole group goes with it, which then comes out of a group around it that stays, and a group sent by its header or a drag goes whole, with every session the core has in it then, its groups' too; a name finds a group of the session's own space, and a session never joins another space's group. The groups live in the `@irie_groups` option of the `irie-agents` session and a session's group in the journal (`ui.grouped`), so a restarted core keeps both; a closed session keeps its group and its space for its reopen. A group goes with its last session, open or closed, its groups' included, or when you ungroup it, which leaves its sessions and groups where they are, in the group around it. Joining, leaving, moving and ungrouping are steps of the history, which one undo takes back, the groups around them too, made again should they have gone; renaming one is not. The Repo view groups by repo and leaves your groups out.

**Sub tabs.** A card's helpers and teammates (its subs) hang under it. A × on one's row, under the pointer, hides it from the card for now: it keeps working, and a line, `2 hidden · Show`, brings it back. Stop in its menu ends it (`stop_sub`): a helper runs inside its session's agent, so its tool calls are turned away from then on, each with word to end at once, and it ends at its next step; a teammate's terminal ends. Closing a card ends all its subs: its helpers with its agent, and its teammates' terminals with it.

**Closing many.** Besides one session at a time (§4.5), a card's menu closes the others (every card but the pinned, the parked, another machine's and itself), those below it in its group, or all finished, and a group's header closes its group (never Pinned's or a paired machine's). Every close of several goes to the core as one `close` with `sessions` (or `finished`), and is one step of the history, which one undo takes back (§4.5). Finished means its turn is over (done, idle or exited), nothing of its work goes on (§4.8), no helper or teammate works, and it is neither frozen, pinned nor parked. The words are `:close others`, `:close below` and `:close finished`, which read the List view's order.

**Colour.** While a session works, the Mac app and the phone apps wash its card in a colour of its own, and Irie's status line draws its state in it (§6.3). There are six, spread round the whole colour wheel but the needs red, at one low chroma, so the view stays calm ([MAC.md](MAC.md)). The core gives each session one the first time it lists it, from the top of the list down, and each row carries it as `hue` (1 to 6). So that cards side by side stand apart, the colour sits at least 100° round the wheel from the card just above, and as near opposite as it can. It is not the colour of the card above that, nor of the one above in its repo, and of those choices it is one the fewest sessions have. Parked cards take theirs among the parked. A session keeps its colour wherever it moves. The colours live in the `@irie_hues` option of the `irie-agents` session, as the order does, so a restarted core keeps them, and a closed session keeps its colour for its reopen.

| Key (list focus) | Action |
|---|---|
| `↑` `↓`, `j` `k`, wheel, PgUp/PgDn, Home/End | Move the selection over the cards and headers that show, or scroll. The wheel scrolls the view without moving the selection |
| Enter, click | Show the session in the slot and focus it; on a header, fold or unfold its group |
| Space | Show it, keep focus in the sidebar; on a header, fold or unfold its group |
| `v`, a click on a tab | Switch between the List and Repo views; the selected session stays selected |
| The pointer | Lights the card, header or tab under it; the light goes out at the margins and when the pane loses focus |
| `Ctrl+]` (tmux binding) | Toggle focus between sidebar and slot |
| `Ctrl+\` (tmux binding, `ui.flip_key`) | Flip the slot between the shown session's conversation and its raw terminal (§4.7) |
| Tab, `:` | Focus the command bar (`:` pre-fills a colon) |
| `/` | Fuzzy jump: the list filters as you type, Enter shows the match (M4) |
| `1`–`9` | Jump to the nth visible row (M4) |
| `r` | Rename: the command bar opens with `:rename` and the row's name, to edit; Enter renames it, Esc keeps the name. The name survives restarts of the sidebar and the core (M4), and Claude Code's titles leave it be (§4.2) |
| `z` | Park or unpark; `Z` collapses or expands Parked (M4) |
| `x` | Close the session (M4, §4.5), at once, as ⌘W does in the Mac app: `u` brings it back. `:close` does the same, and `:reopen` brings back the session closed last; `:close others`, `:close below` and `:close finished` close several as one step (§4.1) |
| `u`, `U` | Undo, redo (M4, §4.5). When the core asks first, a small menu covers the bottom of the list, in a box edged in the accent where it fits, with only whole cards above it: the question, then numbered options, the recommended one first and marked so, the highlighted one on the selection fill with an edge; `1`–`4` or the arrows and Enter pick, Esc cancels, and the status strip says what the highlighted option will do |
| `p`, `P` | Pause or resume this agent, or the whole tree (M5) |
| `f`, `F` | Freeze or thaw this session, or all (M6). Thawing them all takes a second `F`, as each agent signs in again, and they thaw one every 30 s (§4.3); any other key cancels |
| `!`, `n` | Toggle priority; write a note (M7) |
| Drag a card or row (mouse) | Put the session where it is dropped, among the others of its group (in the List view, among the sessions of no group of yours too), or across the Parked group's edge, which parks or unparks it; dropped on another group, it goes back. Held on the list's first or last two lines, or past them, it scrolls the list that way, faster on the edge's own line, and comes along. `:group NAME` and `:ungroup` move it into and out of your groups A click opens a card, row or header when the button comes up on it |
| `m` `m` | Move the selected session under Outside Irie into Irie (§4.6). The first press says what the move ends; any other key cancels |
| `+`, `-` | Rate the session's work good or bad, which teaches the router (M10, §6.4) |

| Edge case | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Pane shorter than 6 rows | Header 1 line, command bar 1 line, at least 1 list row; the status strip is hidden |
| Pane shorter than 10 rows | No tabs, and the command bar has no box |
| A list shorter than 8 rows, or a pane narrower than 24 columns | One-line rows instead of cards. Typing never turns cards into rows: the rule counts a one-line command bar |
| 200 or more sessions | Only visible rows are rendered |
| Name wider than the sidebar | Cut with an ellipsis; the status strip, or the details card's border, shows as much of it as fits |
| Folding a group while one of its sessions is selected | The selection moves to the group's header |
| Two clients of different sizes | `window-size latest`; the slot follows, and agent windows are resized to match |
| The shown session exits | Its pane stays in the slot with its exit status; the row shows exited |
| The selected session disappears | The selection moves to the next row |
| Collapsing Parked while a parked row is selected | The selection moves to the Parked header |

| Failure | Effect | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| `irie ui` crashes | Sidebar blank for under 2 s | The supervisor restarts it. All state is in the core |
| Core crashes | Header says `core offline` | The supervisor restarts the core, which rebuilds from tmux options, `state.json` and gate files |
| Slot pane killed by hand | Layout invariant broken | The core recreates the placeholder within a tick |

**Tests:** model tests in M1 and M4 (pinned header and command bar at three heights, scrolling, park counts, jump ranking), the grouping in both views, folding, hover, clicks on tabs, headers and cards, the fallback to rows, and every cell on the palette, plus tmux integration tests for the swap-in.

### 4.2 Command bar and spawning

| Input | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Plain text | A new task, kept verbatim, with no length cap; or, when it reads as more for the jot just sent, a message to that jot's agent ("More for the last jot", below) |
| `@name`, `@path` | Run in a new worktree of that git repo (M3). A path that is not a git repo runs in place. M1 accepts only absolute or `~` paths and runs in place |
| `@name!` | Run in the repo's main checkout, without a worktree (M3) |
| `+profile` | Use that profile instead of `spawn.default_profile`, which is `auto` (M3; `+auto` and `+ollama:<model>` M10), when the word stands at the start or the end of the first line ("Plus words", below). A profile with a `folder` is an agent of your own: its task runs in that folder, in place, where the agent's CLAUDE.md, skills and records are, unless the task names a place with `@` |
| `+explain`, `+teach` | Run this one task in that tutor mode, beside any `+profile` (`+sonnet +teach …`): Claude Code's Explanatory or Learning output style. The words are reserved: a profile cannot take either name ([learning.md](proposals/learning.md)) |
| `+anon`, `+anon:name`, `+tor`, `+tor:name` | Run this one task as anonymous research, beside any `+profile`: in a compartment of its own, or the named one, which tasks share, leaving through a Mullvad exit of its own, or Tor (§4.16). The words are reserved as profile names too |
| (the Mac app's sheet) | A model for this one task, `model` in `spawn`: an alias Claude Code's `--model` takes (`fable`, `opus`, `sonnet`) or a model's full name. Only a Claude Code profile takes it; a core that does lists `spawn-model` in `hello` |
| (the Mac app's Send to Top) | This one task to the top, `top` in `spawn` and `input`: first in Pinned, and boosted (§4.1, "Send to Top") |
| `:command args` | `:pause [tree\|all]`, `:resume …`, `:freeze [all]`, `:thaw [all]`, `:prio [reason]`, `:note <text>`, `:rename <name>`, `:park`, `:unpark`, `:pin`, `:unpin`, `:group [name]`, `:ungroup`, `:close [others\|below\|finished]`, `:reopen`, `:jump <text>`, `:undo`, `:redo`, `:detach`, `:move [name]` (§4.6). From M10: `:setup [local]` and `:models` open a tool session in the slot; `:setup switch` starts the dead man's switch guide, a Claude Code session on `+claude` in `<data>/guides/dead-mans-switch`, a folder Claude Code then trusts once, that runs Irie's skill `/irie:dead-mans-switch` ([extensions.md](proposals/extensions.md) §2.2); `:write [task]` starts a writing session in Variorum, a Claude Code session on `+claude` in `<data>/guides/variorum` that runs `/irie:variorum` with the task ([writers.md](proposals/writers.md)); with `vpn.on`, `:vpn` says the VPN's state, and `:vpn on`, `:vpn off` and `:vpn use <vpn>` change it, as do, for a VPN that has them, `:vpn lockdown on\|off`, `:vpn time` and `:vpn <place>` (Mullvad's `se`, `se got`, `se-got-wg-001`; a Tailscale exit node; a NordVPN country or city), unless an agent types them (§4.18); `:setup vpn` chooses the VPN; `:rate good\|bad` rates the selected session; `:route <task>` says where `+auto` would run a task; `:open <file>[:line[:col]]` shows a file in a code card beside the selected session, a relative name being its folder's, as `irie ctl open` does for its agent (MAC.md, Code cards); and each word a plugin of yours brings, `:<word> [task]`, starts a session for its app (§4.17) |
| Enter / Shift+Enter or Ctrl+J / Tab / Esc | Submit / new line / complete `@`, `+` or `:` / clear and leave |
| A paste over 8 KiB | Shown as a chip (`[paste 2.1 MB · 31,204 lines]`) and sent intact on submit |

The core reads the bar's text, not the client (`input`, [CORE.md](CORE.md) §3), so every client's bar takes the same words and every `:` command the core knows. The words above are the plan; the core reads `:rename`, `:rate`, `:close`, `:reopen`, `:undo`, `:redo`, `:freeze`, `:thaw`, `:prio`, `:park`, `:unpark`, `:pin`, `:unpin`, `:move`, `:route`, `:setup`, `:models` and `:fork [@TURN] [TASK]` (§4.13), and a status question such as "hows the parser?", which it answers from the rows and which starts nothing. A question that names no session is a task: "hows the plugin pane", with no session of that name, was typed for an agent to look into, so it starts one as any task does, where it once came back refused, with nothing sent. A client that puts a refused task's text back in its bar keeps the notice that says why until the person types.

**Plus words.** A `+` word picks a profile, a tutor mode or anonymous research only when it names one, and only where it stands apart from the task's own words: at the start of the first line or at its end, as in `+sonnet fix it` and `fix it +sonnet`. Every other `+` stays in the task as typed, so a `+` never keeps a task from starting: a lone `+`, as in `fn+x + fn+l`; one before a digit or a sign, as in `+1`, `++` or `+/-`; a `+` word that names nothing, as `+ve` does; and a profile named among the task's words, which the task talks about rather than runs on, as in `why does +dory stall`, which runs on the default. At the end, a word picks only when the line has not named one of its kind before it, so `compare +haiku and +sonnet` picks neither and `+haiku compare it with +sonnet` picks `+haiku`; two picks together, as in `+haiku +sonnet fix it`, are refused. A name matches in any case, without the mark that ends a sentence after it or a colon (`+Haiku,`, `+haiku: fix it`). The owner asked for this on 2026-11-16, after "fn+x + fn+l + fn+r" came back refused twice, "+ needs a profile name", where every `+` word on the first line had been read as a pick. Two rules keep a slip from costing more than a retype. `+anon` and `+tor` pick wherever they stand, so research meant to be anonymous never leaves by the machine's own way out. A word one slip of the fingers from a name, a letter added, left out or changed, or two side by side swapped, as `+hiaku` is from `+haiku`, is refused with the name it meant ("Did you mean +haiku?") where it would pick, and anywhere for `+anon` and `+tor`: a typo would otherwise run the task on the default profile, often the dearest, or outside a compartment. Names of three letters or fewer have too many words a slip away, so they are matched only as typed, but for `+tor`. The core reads the names against its profiles and its peers', `auto`, and the `ollama:` and `llama:` prefixes (`View.Profiles`); a reader that knows no profiles, as `irie ctl spawn` and a `:bench` on a peer, which runs on the peer's, takes every `+` name for one and leaves the core to say which it has. An `@` word picks wherever it stands, as before; a lone `@`, as in `meet @ noon`, is text.

**Spawn pipeline.** Reliable headless spawning means the task never travels through tmux and is never typed into a TUI:
1. Parse the first line: the `@…` words and the `+…` words that pick ("Plus words", above) are removed, and the rest is the task.
2. Resolve the working directory: a worktree (rules below), the main checkout (`@name!`), a plain path, the profile's `folder` in place, or with no token `spawn.default_cwd`. Empty means a new scratch dir under `$XDG_DATA_HOME/irie/scratch/`, so an untargeted task never runs in `$HOME`.
3. Write `sessions/<id>/task.md` and `spec.json` (argv, env, cwd).
4. In one command list, run `new-window -d -t irie-agents: -n <id> -P -F '#{pane_id}' '<irie> host --spec <path>'`, set the pane options and the window size, and name the window after the session.
5. The host calls `setpgid`, makes the agent the foreground process group of its terminal, execs it, then waits with `WUNTRACED` and writes `exit.json`.
6. The agent receives the task as an argument (§3.3).
7. Readiness (M2): if `SessionStart` and `UserPromptSubmit` arrive within `spawn.ready_timeout`, the session is working. If the pane dies first, it is failed, with the exit code and the last 5 screen lines. If neither happens, it is stalled (red), with the same tail; a first-run trust prompt or a login prompt is the usual cause. A resumed conversation (§4.6) waits for input, so `SessionStart` with source `resume` alone makes it ready, and idle.
8. The journal records `spawn.requested`, then `session.started` or `spawn.failed`.

`irie ctl spawn` runs `irie up` first, so a script can spawn with no window open.

**Preferences.** `spawn.preferences` is how the user likes agents to work, a line each, such as how short a text to them should be. Every Claude Code session is told them in its SessionStart reply (§3.3), after the delegation note when it gets one (§6.4): `[Irie] The user’s preferences, which always apply:`, then a `- ` line for each. SessionStart comes again after `/clear`, a compaction, a resume and a fork, so they hold all session long. The built-in list has one, from the owner's ask that agents text them in fewer, plainer words: a text or notification to the user reads at a glance on a phone, the point or the ask first, then no more than three short lines, and anything longer goes on a page whose link the text carries. The user's own list replaces it and `[]` tells nothing; the Mac app edits it in Settings > Agents. It takes at most 15 lines of at most 400 characters, so that with the delegation note and a queued note the reply stays within the 10,000 characters Claude Code passes on whole: past that it saves the text to a file and passes a 2,000-character preview. A generic harness has no SessionStart, so its agents are not told.

**More for the last jot.** Context remembered a moment after a task is sent belongs to that task's agent, not to a new one. A task typed to `input`, in a task bar, the command bar or with `irie ctl input`, is a jot (`jot` in `spawn`, which `input` sets). It goes to the agent of the jot sent just before it, as a message, when all of these hold:
- it comes within `spawn.follow_up_window` (2 minutes) of that jot, or of the last jot added to it;
- it asks for the same place and agent: no `@repo`, folder, `+profile`, model or name of its own, unless the same, and no `+anon` or `+tor` of its own, unless for the same named compartment: a jot that names none goes on in the compartment of the jot before (§4.16);
- it is not sent to the top: one sent there is a task of its own (§4.1, "Send to Top");
- that jot is still starting, or its agent is still there: not ended, failed or closed;
- it reads as more for that jot. It starts with words that go on from it (`also`, `and`, `oh`, `plus`, `btw`, `PS`, `actually`, `I forgot`, `I meant`, `make sure`, `don't`, `one more thing`, `same`, `then`, `more context`, `update:`, `note:` …) or point back at it (`it`, `it's`, `that`, `this`, `these`, `they`, `here's`, `for that`, `^` …), has `also` among its first four words, or is only a link, a path or an error message (`TypeError: …`, `panic: …`, `fatal: …`). A word such as `update` or `note` counts only with its colon, so "update the README" is a task of its own. `don't` or `never` before `think`, `know` or another word of the writer's own mind is "I don't think" with its "I" left out, the writer's view of anything: the words after it decide, so "dont think it worked" adds and "dont think the windows download worked, what's the command" is a task of its own.

Anything else starts a session, as before, and is the jot the next may add to: "deep research X" sent nine seconds after "deep research Y" starts a second agent. The words are read the same way every time and cost no tokens (`internal/jot`). A `spawn` without `jot`, as from `irie ctl spawn` or delegation, never adds.

`input` answers `did: "added"`, "Added to …", with the session and `until`: the jot waits `spawn.follow_up_delay` (8 s) before it is typed, so that the same text sent again with `apart` naming that session takes it back and starts it as a task of its own (`spawn`'s own `added`, `until`, `apart` and `late` underneath). The Mac app's notice offers that as **New task**; when the agent had it already, the reply says it has it too. A jot added while the one before is still being spawned waits for that spawn, and fails with it. The core types an added jot as `send` does a message (§4.7), once the agent can take one: not while it starts, waits on you in its terminal, or is frozen; and one of several lines only once it asks for bracketed paste. Claude Code takes what is typed while it works at its next tool call. Jots waiting for an agent are kept in its session folder (`followups.json`), so a core that restarts meanwhile still types them, and are dropped when the session ends; a session that closes and is reopened starts with none.

**Names.** A session's name is what its row shows (`@irie_name`). Its slug, the first five words of the task's first line in lower case joined by `-` (`fix-the-flaky-login-test`), names its scratch dir, worktree and branch.
- **Titles.** A task given no name is named after its first line: the `@` and `+` tokens that may lead it left out, whitespace made single spaces, in title case and cut at a word's end to 40 characters with `…` (`Fix the Flaky Login Test`). Claude Code then gets no `--name`, so it titles the session itself from the first prompt, and again when a plan is accepted (Claude Code's sessions docs). So the title comes from the task's subject and is not refreshed as the conversation moves on: in local runs of 2.1.310, later prompts on other subjects brought no new title. Each title is an `ai-title` line in the session's own transcript, not a subagent's, which the core reads with the token tally (§4.7); a new one renames the row, numbered when another row has the name. A name that is the title already, numbered or not, stays, so a restarted core, which reads every transcript again, changes nothing. A title is not a rename: nothing is journaled and undo never sees it. Titles that a `custom-title` line follows, from a name given in Claude Code itself, are passed over. Where no titles come, as from another harness, a model that cannot make them or `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_TERMINAL_TITLE`, the first name stays. A session moved in (§4.6) starts with the name Claude Code lists it under.
- **Title case.** Every name Irie makes, from a task or from Claude Code's title, is in title case, as the AMA Manual of Style has it: the first and last word of a title, and of a part that a colon ends or starts, capitalised, and every other word too but articles (`a`, `an`, `the`), the conjunctions Chicago keeps in lower case (`and`, `but`, `for`, `or`, `nor`) and the prepositions of three letters or fewer that are seldom adverbs or nouns in a task (`as`, `at`, `by`, `in`, `of`, `on`, `per`, `to`, `via`). A word with a capital, a digit or any of `/`, `.`, `_` and `` ` ``, or a flag's leading `-`, stays as written: `iOS`, `README`, `main.go`, `--verbose`. A name the user gives is never recased.
- **The user's name.** A name given at spawn (`name`, `irie ctl spawn --name`) or by `rename` is the user's: shown as given, but for whitespace, and pinned in `@irie_named`, so titles leave it be, through restarts, close and reopen. A spawn passes it with `--name`, and its slug names the folder. A failover carries the name on, as the user's only when it was.
- **Sessions from before titles** have no `@irie_named`, and when closed no `named` in `ui.closed`. The core settles each once, when it adopts or reopens it: a name that is its task's slug, or that numbered, becomes the task's title, and any other, given with `--name`, by `rename` or by a move, is kept as the user's.

**Worktree rules (M3).**

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Location | `worktree.root/<repo>/<slug>` (default `$XDG_DATA_HOME/irie/worktrees`). Outside the repo, so tools that scan the repo never see nested checkouts and Claude Code does not load the main checkout's CLAUDE.md a second time from a parent directory |
| Branch | `worktree.branch_prefix` + slug, e.g. `irie/fix-flaky-test`; `-2`, `-3` on collision. Based on `worktree.base`; when that is unset, on the repo's land branch (`dog.land`, §4.8) if the repo has it, so a task starts from the work that has landed, and else on the main checkout's `HEAD` |
| Untracked local files | A worktree holds tracked files only, so a repo's untracked local settings never arrive on their own. Irie copies, when present: every path matched by the repo's `.worktreeinclude` (gitignore syntax, the convention Claude Code and others use), plus `worktree.copy` (default `.claude/settings.local.json` and `CLAUDE.local.md`). The status strip lists what was copied |
| Submodules | `git submodule update --init --recursive` when `.gitmodules` exists (`worktree.submodules`) |
| Dirty main checkout | Uncommitted changes are not carried over, and the status strip says so |
| Repo names | `@name` resolves through `repos.aliases`, then git repos found two levels under `repos.roots`, then recently used repos. An ambiguous name lists the candidates instead of guessing |
| Removal | Never automatic in v1 |

| Edge case or failure | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `claude` not on PATH | The pane exits with 127, and the session is failed with the shell's message |
| A flag value rejected (e.g. `auto` unknown to this CLI or account) | Failed, with the CLI's own message. No silent fallback to another mode |
| Trust or login prompt at first start | Stalled (red). Swap it in and answer. Claude Code keys trust on a repo's root, and in a worktree on its main checkout's root, so trusting a repo once covers every worktree of it (checked in M3) |
| Two tasks with the same first words | Slugs `x` and `x-2` |
| Core down when a spawn is requested | `irie up` restarts it; spawn ids make retries idempotent |
| The task names a repo that does not exist | Nothing is spawned; the status strip lists the closest names |

**Tests:** argv and task-size goldens (M1); readiness, stall and failure via the fake agent (M2); worktree contents and grammar (M3).

### 4.3 Pause, freeze, priority and context notes

| | Pause | Freeze | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scope | One agent (`main` or one subagent), one tree, or all | A session's whole process tree, or all sessions | One session |
| Takes effect | At the next tool boundary | At once | Badge at once, and no card moves; the note at the next tool boundary or prompt |
| Mechanism | The hook shim waits | The core sends SIGSTOP, later SIGCONT; the host leaves the stops alone | The other sessions' trees in the background, but their agents; first turn in Irie's queues; the notes channel |
| What the agent is told | On release: "held for X" | After thaw, at its next tool boundary, and only if it was working: "frozen for X" | A steering note |
| Keys | `p` (row), `P` (tree), `:pause all` | `f`, `F` (all) | `!`, `:prio <reason>` |

**Pause.**
- **Gate state.** Each session has `{main, subs{agent_id}, tree}`, held in the core and mirrored to `sessions/<id>/gate.json`. The file is written before the row shows "pausing".
- **Holding.** Every PreToolUse, PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure hook asks the core. If the calling agent is gated, the core keeps the request open; a payload with no `agent_id` comes from `main`. The row changes from "pausing" to "paused" and shows the held tool.
- **Release.** Release answers every waiting shim with the `notes.held` text and any notes queued meanwhile.
- **`hold.max` (default 24 h).** The shim releases itself just before the harness timeout. On PreToolUse it denies the call with: "[Irie] Still paused by the user; this call did not run. Retry the same call to keep waiting." The retry is held again. The harness timeout is `hold.max` + 2 min with `onFailure: "block"`, so a timeout never lets a call through.
- **Core down.** The shim polls `gate.json` every 500 ms: while gated it keeps holding; with no gate it returns at once.

**Freeze.**
- **Who signals.** The core, never the host. It never sends SIGUSR1 or SIGUSR2 to a host: a host from before freeze handles only SIGHUP, SIGTERM and SIGINT, so either signal would end it and its agent with it. The host is never stopped either: tmux continues a stopped pane process at once (checked on a private server), and the host would then continue its agent.
- **Stop.** The core writes `sessions/<id>/freeze.json`, then reads the process table (darwin: `sysctl kern.proc.all` via `golang.org/x/sys/unix`; linux: `/proc`). It stops the agent's process group with one SIGSTOP, then every process under the host outside that group, parents before children, so that no running parent sees a child of its stop. It reads the table again until a pass finds nothing new, at most 5 passes. `freeze.on` records the pids, and the newest start time in the table, so that what starts later can be told apart.
- **Hosts from before freeze.** A host says in `host.json` that it leaves its agent stopped while `freeze.json` exists. A host without one, such as those of agents that ran before the install, would continue its agent at once. So there the agent runs on and everything under it stops, and the hook shim holds the agent at its next tool call: at PreToolUse, PostToolUse and PostToolUseFailure it waits while `freeze.json` exists, with the core down too. The row says "freezing" until the agent reaches one. Each tick stops what started in a frozen tree after the freeze, such as the shim itself, and never continues anything.
- **Thaw.** The core continues every stopped process under the host, children before parents, then removes `freeze.json`. A host that reads a stale stop report only sends a harmless SIGCONT.
- **Restarts.** Frozen comes from the journal's `freeze.on` and `freeze.off`, so a frozen session stays frozen through a core restart, and the core never continues it on its own. A reopened session starts unfrozen.
- **Stops the host did not cause.** The host continues them within 100 ms, for example the agent's own Ctrl+Z handling, so an agent never wedges; while the session is frozen it leaves them. tmux does the same for its direct child, which is why the agent runs under a host and not as the pane's first process.
- **Input.** While a session is frozen the core turns off its pane's input (`select-pane -d`), so keystrokes don't pile up, and turns it back on at thaw.
- **Long freezes.** After `freeze.warn_after` the row warns that network streams may have dropped. A freeze longer than the session's prompt cache lives costs a rebuild once the thaw wakes it: the row's `cold` says the price, which the Mac app's Resume says in its help (§6.3).
- **Thawing several.** A thaw of more than one session, of them all (`F`, `:thaw all`, `thaw` with `all`) or an undo or redo of such a step (§4.5), thaws them in turn: the first at once, then one every 30 s after Irie's last thaw, the trod bot's included (§4.10), so that each agent frozen long finds the sign-in the one before renewed. A turn that fails for want of a sign-in (`authentication_failed`) after the thaw began holds the rest frozen until it is seen to. Their rows say "Thaws in turn, one session every 30 s", or what holds them. `thaw` answers `sessions[]`, those thawed now, and `queued[]`, those to come. `state.json` keeps the queue, so a restarted core goes on with it. A freeze or a thaw of one of them by hand takes it out of the queue; undoing the step freezes again those it thawed and keeps the rest frozen.
- **Close while frozen.** SIGCONT to everything the host runs, the host last, then SIGTERM through the host, then SIGKILL after 5 s. A stopped process that catches SIGTERM, as Claude Code does, only handles it once it runs again, so the host also continues the agent's group whenever it passes a signal on.

**Priority.**
- **Level.** 0 or 1, with an optional reason. The field is an integer, so more levels can come later. The UIs call level 1 "boosted" (Boost, Stop Boosting), which earlier builds called promoted; the op is `priority`, and `irie ctl boost` sets it, as `irie ctl promote` still does. A task sent to the top starts at 1 (§4.1, "Send to Top").
- **Order.** A boost changes the priority alone and never moves a card: the list's order is the user's (drag, move, pin, park; §4.1). Boosting a parked session leaves it in Parked, and undoing a boost gives back only the level (§4.5). Undoing a step from before, which kept where a promotion had moved the session from, gives back only the level too, and no card moved when this came in.
- **The machine first.** Without root no process can be raised above normal priority, and a nice value, once raised, can only be lowered again by root (setpriority(2), renice(8)). So while any session is promoted, the core puts every other session's process tree in macOS's background state, `setpriority(PRIO_DARWIN_PROCESS, pid, PRIO_DARWIN_BG)` as `taskpolicy -b -p` does: the lowest CPU priority, throttled disk I/O, and throttled network I/O on sockets opened from then on. The promoted trees stay as they are, and so does the tab the user is on while they are at the computer with Gabriel on (§4.11, the tab on top), which comes before a promotion: while it works the machine, the promoted trees step back for it too. Each tick puts what those trees start in the background too, which it also inherits. Putting the session back takes every process of the other trees out of the background, and a process that left a tree, such as a daemon, comes out as soon as it leaves. It takes effect before the op returns. Linux has no such state that a user can undo, so there priority orders the list and Irie's queues only.
- **Irie's queues.** What Irie hands out one at a time serves a promoted session first, then the rest in the order they asked: spawns with their worktrees (a session's own, as `parent`, when it delegates), and messages typed to agents (`send`). A local model's server queues its own requests, which Irie cannot reorder.
- **The agent keeps its network.** macOS's background state throttles a process's network as well as its CPU and disk: an agent in it gets its API reply in a trickle, and a long reply fails with "API Error: The response stopped arriving", which the row shows as "turn failed: server_error". With 11 sessions promoted, every other session's agent once sat at `ps -o pri=` 4 and took 5 to 15 minutes a step. So in a tree that steps back, for a promotion, for Gabriel or for the tab on top (§4.11), the agent stays at normal priority: the program the pane's `irie host` runs (the host, the pane's first process, never steps back either), and Irie's own helpers that the agent runs, with what they start. Those are its hook calls, which Claude Code gives up on after their timeout, 10 s for most, so that one starved in the background loses its event; its status line; and Irie's browser connector (§4.15) with the browser it drives, which waits on the network for the agent as the agent's own web fetches do, and runs only while the agent browses. Everything else in the tree steps back: the agent's builds, tests and other tools, and MCP servers of others. A tool the agent starts comes in at normal priority, as the agent runs, and steps back at the core's next tick, within a second; what the tool starts in turn inherits the background. A held-back session's `claude` shows 31 under `ps -o pri=`, its tools 4. The core never puts these in the background, and takes them out once as it finds a tree, from where a core before it may have left them. The core tells the helpers by name, the program's own name in the process table (`kern.proc.all`'s `p_comm`), among the agent's direct children.
- **A boost after API errors.** When a session's turn fails on an API error that may pass twice in a row (`server_error`, `overloaded`, `unknown`, `max_output_tokens`, the errors after which the trod bot tries a turn again, §4.10), the core boosts it: its whole tree comes out of the background, as a boosted session's does, and no other session steps back for it. It ranks with the rest of the user's focus: while the tab on top works the machine, it steps back too, all but its agent (§4.11). Its card stays where it is, with no badge, and its level stays 0; the card says "Boosted after two API errors" before what it does. Two seconds on, the core types its agent one message, "[Irie] Your turn ended on an API error. Carry on where you stopped.", as `continue` types its word (§4.7): only if the agent still waits on that turn, nothing was sent to it since, and its prompt shows with nothing typed in it; Enter follows as for `send`, since the message may wrap in a narrow terminal. The prompt that follows counts as Irie's, not the user's, as the trod bot's do. The boost ends with the first turn that ends without an API error, at its Stop or the stop the jot dog or the trod bot sends on, or after 15 minutes at most. Only the main agent's failures count. An error only the user can mend, such as a usage limit or a login, breaks the run of failures, and failures during a boost count toward no other: two more after it ends start one. The journal keeps `boost.on` and `boost.off` (§5.2), so a restarted core goes on with a boost and counts the failures before it. A turn that failed while no core listened counts too, once the core takes it back (§3.3).
- **Memory.** macOS gives a user's process no way to set memory aside for another, so a boost reserves none. The apps say so. The background processes are the ones macOS slows first.
- **Notes.** Raising priority queues `notes.priority`. Clearing it sends `notes.priority_cleared`, but only if the first note was delivered.
- **Paused or frozen sessions.** Priority does not resume them; the status strip says "paused, p to resume".

**Notes channel (how agents stay "context aware").**
- **Queue.** Notes queue per session and agent.
- **Delivery.** A note goes out at the agent's next PreToolUse, PostToolUse or PostToolUseFailure as `additionalContext`, or with its next prompt (UserPromptSubmit) if the agent is idle. Each note is delivered at most once.
- **Generic harnesses.** Bracketed paste plus Enter, and only while the session is not frozen.
- **Size.** Notes are at most 2,000 characters. Longer text goes to `sessions/<id>/note-<n>.md`, and the note points to that file.

| Edge case | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Pause while the agent is thinking or streaming | "pausing" until its next tool boundary |
| Pause an idle agent | "pausing (idle)": the first tool call of the next turn is held |
| A long-running tool (build, tests) | Held right after it finishes, at PostToolUse |
| Parallel tool calls | Each call is held; one release frees them all |
| Typing into a held agent | Claude Code holds typed input until the hook returns. To steer a held agent, send a note: it arrives with the release |
| Esc in a held agent | Claude Code cancels the hook. The gate stays set, so the next tool call is held again |
| Pause plus freeze | Both are shown; thawing returns the session to paused |
| A gated subagent finishes | Its gate is removed with it |
| Freeze while a hold is waiting | The shim is frozen too; thawing resumes the wait |
| A process that left the tree (a daemon re-parented to init or launchd) | Not frozen; listed in the status strip |

| Failure | Effect | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Core crashes during a hold | The shim keeps holding from `gate.json` | The new core reloads its gates |
| Shim killed or crashed | PreToolUse with `onFailure: "block"` blocks the call | The model retries and is held again |
| Claude Code older than 2.1.295 | Pause would leak on timeout | Pause is disabled for that session, and the row says why |
| No host (a pane Irie did not start) | Nothing to signal | Freeze is disabled for that row |
| An API stream times out during a freeze | Claude Code retries on its own | The frozen note tells the model to re-check its state |

**Tests:** M5, M6 and M7.

### 4.4 Subagent panes

1. **Start.** On `SubagentStart` (`agent_id`, `agent_type`) Irie adds a child row under the parent and opens a mirror window in `irie-agents` running `irie mirror --session <id> --agent <agent_id>`. The mirror pane's input is off (`select-pane -d`).
2. **Content.**
   - **Sources.** Live tool activity comes from hook events for that `agent_id`, through `subscribe`. Text comes from the subagent transcript at `<dir of transcript_path>/<session_id>/subagents/agent-<agent_id>.jsonl`; `agent_transcript_path` at SubagentStop confirms the path.
   - **Rendering.** The mirror shows assistant text, tool calls (`→ Bash  go test ./...`) and results (`← ok 1.2 s`, errors in red), with timestamps. A status line on top says working, paused or finished.
3. **Finish.** On `SubagentStop` the row shows finished. The mirror stays for `subagents.keep_finished` (default 10 min), then closes. Finished subagents collapse into one `✓ 3 finished` child line.
4. **Row actions.** A subagent row supports show (swap the mirror in), `p` (pause that subagent only) and `n` (a note delivered through that subagent's own tool hooks).

| Edge case or failure | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| The transcript does not exist yet | The mirror shows hook events only and retries every 500 ms |
| Many subagents | Beyond `ui.max_sub_rows` (6), rows collapse into `+4 more` |
| Unknown or malformed transcript lines | Skipped. The format is observed, not a published schema |
| The tailer fails | The mirror shows the error and keeps showing hook events |
| The parent session is closed | Its mirrors close |

**Helpers beside their session.** Built ahead of the rest of M8, after docs/proposals/split-pane.md, which replaces the mirror windows above with views Irie draws. A helper has no terminal of its own, so a view shows one from its own transcript, `agent-<id>.jsonl` in the `subagents` folder beside its session's. `conversation` and `subscribe` with `agent: "sub:<id>"` read it with the reader of §4.7, which keeps its sidechain lines and marks its first message `from: "parent"`, the task its session gave it. An agent id is checked before it names a file: letters, digits, `-` and `_` only. An `agent` item names its helper as `agent` once the call's result gives the id, which a subagent in the background returns at once. A subagent still running in the foreground gets the running helper of its kind that no other item names, the oldest first. `ui.helper_style` picks the look, `browser` (cards and plain words) or `terminal`, and the header carries it to every view. The Mac app shows a helper in its session's Helpers card, in the panes beside the raw terminal or the conversation, whichever shows ([MAC.md](MAC.md), docs/proposals/workspaces.md §4); the right sidebar's Helpers tab is the user's own helpers (§4.17), the same for every session; an agent team's teammates, which have terminals of their own, stand beside the session's terminal instead (§2.3). Nothing in tmux changes, so no agent is resized.

**Tests:** M8; for the helpers beside their session, `internal/core/helpers_test.go`.

### 4.5 Undo and redo

| Action | Inverse | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Rename | Rename back | The name before comes back as it was: the user's, or one Claude Code's titles change (`before_named`). Its label names both names, `rename "a" to "b"`. Asks when the session was renamed since or another took the name |
| Park / unpark | Unpark / park | The step keeps the sessions that came after it before the park and after: undo puts it back in the place it left, and redo where the park put it. Asks when the session is closed or gone (skip), or is that way already (settle). A park that changed nothing, and promote's unpark, record no step |
| Reorder | Back to its place | The step keeps the three sessions that came after it, before the move and after; it goes back just before the first of them still in the order, or last |
| Priority on / off | Off / on | Undoing a boost gives back the level and nothing else, as a boost moves no card; undoing "stop boosting" boosts it again. Cancels a note that has not been delivered. If it was delivered, sends `notes.priority_cleared` |
| Pause / resume | Resume / pause | Releasing still sends the "held for" note |
| Freeze / thaw | Thaw / freeze | One session, or them all at once (`F`) as one step whose label names the sessions it changed, `freeze "a", "b" and "c"`, past four the first three and how many more. What the trod bot freezes and thaws records no step |
